📺 Watch Lecture Photolithography: Photolithography Steps & Process in VLSI (Engineering Funda) ↗

page 105

# 4. Photolithography —

Photolithography is the process used to transfer a geometric pattern from a photomask onto a photosensitive material (photoresist) on the wafer surface.

# Steps of Photolithography —

  1. Wafer cleaning.
  2. Photoresist coating (spin coating).
  3. Soft baking (prebake).
  4. Mask alignment & exposure (UV light).
  5. Development.
  6. Hard baking (postbake).
  7. Etching.
  8. Photoresist removal (stripping).
Fig: Photolithography sequence — wafer w/ SiO2 → photoresist coat → mask exposure (UV) → development → etched pattern → resist strip
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Photolithography sequence                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • wafer w/ SiO2 → photoresist coat → mask exposure (UV)  │
│   → development → etched pattern → resist strip          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Page 106
📺 Watch Lecture Photolithography: Photolithography Steps & Process in VLSI (Engineering Funda) ↗

page 106

# Types of Photoresist —

Positive photoresistNegative photoresist
Exposed areas become soluble and are washed away in development.Exposed areas become insoluble (polymerize/harden) and remain after development.
Pattern on wafer = same as mask pattern.Pattern on wafer = reverse of mask pattern.
Better resolution, widely used in modern VLSI.Lower resolution, cheaper, more durable.

# 5. Etching —

* Etching removes unwanted material (oxide/poly/metal) from the areas not protected by photoresist, to transfer the pattern into the underlying layer.

* Two types:

  1. Wet etching — uses chemical solutions (acids), isotropic (etches sideways too), lower cost, less precise for very small features.
  2. Dry etching (Plasma/RIE) — uses reactive ion/plasma, anisotropic (vertical etch only), high precision, used for sub-micron features.
Fig: Wet etching vs Dry/RIE etching — Wet etching (isotropic, undercut under mask) vs Dry/RIE etching (anisotropic, vertical walls)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Wet etching vs Dry/RIE etching                   │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • Wet etching (isotropic, undercut under mask) vs        │
│   Dry/RIE etching (anisotropic, vertical walls)          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Page 107
📺 Watch Lecture Ion Implantation: Ion Implantation & Diffusion in IC Fabrication (TekNotes) ↗

page 107

# 6. Diffusion —

* Diffusion is the process of introducing dopant atoms (impurities) into the silicon wafer by heating the wafer in presence of dopant gas/source at high temperature (900–1200°C), so that dopants move into the silicon by thermal diffusion.

* Used to create n-type or p-type regions (source, drain, well).

Fig: Diffusion furnace — dopant gas source, wafers in quartz tube, dopant concentration profile decreasing with depth
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Diffusion furnace                                │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • dopant gas source, wafers in quartz tube, dopant       │
│   concentration profile decreasing with depth            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

# 7. \(I_{\text{on}}\) Implantation —

* \(I_{\text{on}}\) implantation is a process of doping in which dopant ions are accelerated by high voltage (as an ion beam) and directly bombarded/implanted into the silicon wafer.

# Advantages of \(I_{\text{on}}\) implantation over Diffusion —

  1. Precise control over dopant concentration and depth (dose & energy controlled).
  2. Low temperature process (room temperature), unlike diffusion (high temp).
  3. Better uniformity across wafer.
  4. Can implant through thin oxide/masking layers.
Page 108

page 108

# 8. Metallization —

* Metallization is the process of depositing a metal layer (usually Aluminium or Copper) onto the wafer to form interconnections between different devices on the chip, and to form contact pads for external connections.

# Methods —

  1. Physical Vapor Deposition (PVD) / Sputtering.
  2. Chemical Vapor Deposition (CVD).
  3. Electroplating (used mainly for Cu interconnects).
Fig: Metallization — metal layer deposited over interlayer dielectric, patterned by photolithography+etch to form interconnect lines contacting source/drain/gate
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Metallization                                    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • metal layer deposited over interlayer dielectric,      │
│   patterned by photolithography+etch to form             │
│   interconnect lines contacting source/drain/gate        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

# 9. Passivation —

* Passivation is the final step where a protective layer (e.g. Si3N4 or SiO2) is deposited over the entire chip to protect it from moisture, contamination, and mechanical damage.

* Openings (vias) are left only over the bond pads for wire bonding.

Page 109
📺 Watch Lecture CMOS n-Well Fabrication: CMOS Transistor n-Well Fabrication Process Flow (Education 4u) ↗

page 109

# CMOS Fabrication Process (n-well process) —

The n-well CMOS process is the most common process used to fabricate CMOS ICs, where PMOS transistors are built inside an n-well on a p-type substrate.

# Steps —

  1. Start with p-type substrate.
  2. Define n-well region by photolithography, implant/diffuse n-type dopants to form n-well.
  3. Grow field oxide (thick SiO2) for isolation between devices (LOCOS process).
  4. Grow thin gate oxide in active regions.
  5. Deposit & pattern polysilicon to form gate electrodes.
Fig: n-well CMOS cross-section (step 1) — p-substrate with n-well region formed on one side, field oxide isolating regions
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : n-well CMOS cross-section (step 1)               │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • p-substrate with n-well region formed on one side,     │
│   field oxide isolating regions                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Page 110
📺 Watch Lecture CMOS n-Well Fabrication: CMOS Transistor n-Well Fabrication Process Flow (Education 4u) ↗

page 110

  1. Implant n+ source/drain regions in p-substrate (NMOS) using self-aligned poly-gate as mask.
  2. Implant p+ source/drain regions in n-well (PMOS) using self-aligned poly-gate as mask.
  3. Deposit interlayer dielectric (ILD, SiO2) over entire wafer.
  4. Etch contact holes (vias) down to source/drain/gate/well regions.
  5. Deposit & pattern metal layer to form interconnections.
  6. Deposit passivation layer, open bond pad windows.
Fig: Completed n-well CMOS inverter cross-section — NMOS (n+ S/D in p-sub) and PMOS (p+ S/D in n-well) connected by metal, poly gates, field oxide isolation, contacts to \(V_{DD}\)/GND/Out
Page 111

page 111

# Latch-up in CMOS —

Latch-up is a phenomenon in CMOS circuits in which parasitic bipolar transistors (formed inherently by the n-well/p-substrate structure) turn on and create a low-resistance path between \(V_{DD}\) and GND, causing excessive current flow that can permanently damage the chip.

Fig: Parasitic bipolar transistors — pnp (in n-well) and npn (in p-substrate) forming a parasitic SCR structure between \(V_{DD}\) and GND
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Parasitic bipolar transistors                    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • pnp (in n-well) and npn (in p-substrate) forming a     │
│   parasitic SCR structure between VDD and GND            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

# Causes of Latch-up —

  1. Voltage spikes / noise on \(V_{DD}\) or GND rails.
  2. Overshoot/undershoot of input signals beyond supply rails.
  3. High substrate/well resistance allowing parasitic BJTs to trigger.
Page 112

page 112

# Prevention of Latch-up —

  1. Guard rings — placing heavily doped n+ and p+ guard rings around n-well and NMOS regions respectively, tied to \(V_{DD}\)/GND to collect stray carriers.
  2. Reducing well and substrate resistance (lower resistivity substrate/epitaxial layer).
  3. Increasing spacing between NMOS and PMOS devices.
  4. Using retrograde well profiles (heavier doping deeper in the well).
  5. Using Silicon-on-Insulator (SOI) technology to eliminate the parasitic structure entirely.
Fig: Guard ring layout — n+ guard ring around n-well tied to \(V_{DD}\), p+ guard ring around NMOS tied to GND, placed between NMOS and PMOS devices
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Guard ring layout                                │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • n+ guard ring around n-well tied to VDD, p+ guard ring │
│   around NMOS tied to GND, placed between NMOS and PMOS  │
│   devices                                                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Interface with Fabrication house

* CIF (Caltech Intermediate Form) Code

CIF is a low level graphics language for specifying the geometry of integrated circuit. The purpose of CIF code is to communicate chip geometry in a standard machine-readable for mask-making.

* Geometric primitives

Various geometric structures such as boxes, polygons and wires are readily defined. In general, the position, dimension, and orientation must be specified and, also of course, the layer on which the box exists in the silicon.

Fig: CIF primitives
Coordinate grid (x: -20 to +120, y: -20 to +120)

- A horizontal rectangle labeled "length" and "center" at ~(20,45)
- A triangle with vertices at (-10,-10), (20,-25), (40,10)
- A wire path (rounded, dashed centerline) from ~(80,120) curving down
- A shaded rectangular polygon region near (80-120, -20 to 20) with 
  points labeled L, K, B, A, F, T, V, E marking a wire/via layer

📺 Watch Lecture Electromigration: Electromigration in VLSI Design & Black's Equation (Team VLSI) ↗

Reliability Issues: Electromigration

What is Electromigration?

Electromigration is the transport of material caused by the gradual movement of the ions in a metal conductor due to the momentum transfer between conducting electrons and the diffusing metal atoms. Over time, this migration of metal atoms leads to wire failure in integrated circuits.

Physics and Mechanism:

When a high current density flows through a sub-micron metal interconnect, two opposing forces act on the metal ions:

  1. Electrostatic Force (\(F_{\text{direct}}\)): The direct pull of the electric field on the positively charged metal ions (pointing from positive to negative terminal).
  2. Wind Force (\(F_{\text{wind}}\)): The momentum transferred by the high-velocity "electron wind" colliding with the metal ions, pushing them in the direction of electron flow. The wind force dominates in metal interconnects.

As ions are dragged along, they accumulate at boundaries or points of current density change (forming Hillocks or whiskers that cause short circuits), while leaving behind empty spaces at their origin (forming Voids that cause open circuits).

Black's Equation for MTF:

The reliability of a wire against electromigration is modeled using Black's Equation, which estimates the Mean Time to Failure (MTF):

\[\text{MTF} = \frac{A}{J^n} \exp\left( \frac{E_a}{k T} \right)\]

Where:

  • \(A\) is a constant based on the cross-sectional area, geometry, and material of the interconnect.
  • \(J\) is the current density in the wire (\(\text{A/cm}^2\)).
  • \(n\) is a scaling factor (typically between 1 and 2, depending on the failure mode).
  • \(E_a\) is the activation energy of metal diffusion (e.g., \(\approx 0.6\text{ eV}\) for Aluminum).
  • \(k\) is Boltzmann's constant (\(8.617 \times 10^{-5}\text{ eV/K}\)).
  • \(T\) is the absolute temperature in Kelvin (K).

Prevention Methods in VLSI Layout:

  • Current Density Limits: Design rules enforce a maximum current density limit (typically \(J \le 1 \text{ mA/}\mu\text{m}^2\)). Wires carrying high currents must be widened accordingly.
  • Copper Doping (Al-Cu): Adding a small fraction (e.g., 0.5%) of Copper to Aluminum wires significantly reduces electromigration by filling grain boundary voids.
  • Pure Copper Metallization: Modern sub-micron chips use Copper instead of Aluminum because Copper has a higher density, better conductivity, and higher activation energy (\(E_a \approx 0.9\text{ eV}\)).
  • Barrier Layers: Encapsulating wires with refractory metals (like Titanium, Tantalum, or Tungsten) prevents surface electromigration.
Fig: Electromigration Mechanism (Void and Hillock Formation)
2021-22 2021-22)1)b [5 Marks] Discuss the photolithography process of CMOS VLSI fabrication.
▼
Sol

Photolithography is the core process of transferring micro-patterns from a mask onto the surface of a silicon wafer. The steps involve:

  1. Oxidation: Growing a thin barrier layer of silicon dioxide (\(\text{SiO}_2\)) on the wafer substrate.
  2. Photoresist Application: Spin-coating a light-sensitive polymer liquid (photoresist) evenly over the wafer.
  3. Soft Bake: Heating to remove solvent residue and harden the photoresist layer.
  4. Mask Alignment and Exposure: Positioning the glass mask containing the layer layout and exposing the wafer to intense Ultraviolet (UV) light. For positive photoresists, exposed areas become highly soluble.
  5. Development: Immersing the wafer in developer solvent to wash away the exposed photoresist, leaving the exact mask layout pattern on the wafer.
  6. Hard Bake: Final heating to ensure maximum adhesion of the remaining resist pattern.
  7. Etching or Implantation: Chemically etching away the exposed \(\text{SiO}_2\) or implanting dopants into the underlying exposed silicon channels.
  8. Stripping: Using chemical solvents or oxygen plasma to cleanly strip away the remaining hardened photoresist.
2021-22 2021-22)1)c [5 Marks] List the steps of CMOS inverter fabrication with appropriate diagrams.
▼
Sol

The fabrication of a CMOS inverter on a p-type substrate via the n-well process proceeds as follows:

  1. n-well Formation: Masking the wafer and implanting n-type impurities (like phosphorus) to create the n-well region where the PMOS transistor will reside.
  2. Active Area Definition: Depositing nitride and using photolithography to define active regions for NMOS and PMOS, isolating them using thick Field Oxide (LOCOS/STI).
  3. Gate Oxide & Polysilicon Deposition: Growing a thin gate oxide layer, depositing polysilicon, and patterning it to form the high-precision gate electrodes for both transistors.
  4. n+ Diffusion: Masking the PMOS active area and implanting n-type impurities (arsenic) to form the NMOS source, drain, and the n-well contact (taps).
  5. p+ Diffusion: Masking the NMOS active area and implanting p-type impurities (boron) to form the PMOS source, drain, and the p-substrate contact (taps).
  6. Metallization: Depositing thick insulating oxide (\(\text{SiO}_2\)), etching contact holes (vias), sputtering a metal layer (aluminum/copper), and etching it to establish \(V_{DD}\), GND, \(V_{\text{in}}\), and \(V_{\text{out}}\) interconnects.
2021-22 2021-22)3)b [4 Marks] Consider an NMOS transistor with \(V_t = 0.8\text{ V}\), \(\beta = 30\,\mu\text{A/V}^2\) and \(W/L = 2\). If \(V_{GS} = 3\text{ V}\) and \(V_{DS} = 3.5\text{ V}\), calculate \(I_{DS}\).
▼
Sol

1. Given parameters:

  • Threshold voltage, \(V_t = 0.8\text{ V}\)
  • Gain factor, \(\beta_0 = \mu_n C_{ox} = 30\,\mu\text{A/V}^2\) (with \(W/L = 2 \implies \beta = 60\,\mu\text{A/V}^2\))
  • Aspect ratio, \(W/L = 2\)
  • Gate-to-source voltage, \(V_{GS} = 3\text{ V}\)
  • Drain-to-source voltage, \(V_{DS} = 3.5\text{ V}\)

2. Determine the Region of Operation:

Compare \(V_{DS}\) with the gate overdrive voltage \((V_{GS} - V_t)\):

\[V_{GS} - V_t = 3.0 - 0.8 = 2.2\text{ V}\]

Since \(V_{DS} = 3.5\text{ V} \ge (V_{GS} - V_t) = 2.2\text{ V}\), the transistor is in the Saturation Region.

3. Calculate Drain Current (\(I_{DS}\)):

The drain current equation in saturation is:

\[I_{DS} = \frac{1}{2}\mu_n C_{ox}\left(\frac{W}{L}\right)(V_{GS} - V_t)^2\]

Substituting the given values:

\[I_{DS} = \frac{30}{2} \times 2 \times (2.2)^2 = 30 \times 4.84 = 145.2\,\mu\text{A}\]

Result: The drain current is 145.2 μA.

2021-22 2021-22)3)c [5 Marks] Consider an NMOS Inverter with an NMOS Enhancement Transistor Load. Calculate \(V_{\text{out}}\) if \(V_{\text{in}} = 0\text{ V}\), \(V_{DD} = 3.3\text{ V}\) and body effect constant \(\gamma = 0.5\text{ V}^{1/2}\).
▼
Sol

When \(V_{\text{in}} = 0\text{ V}\), the enhancement driver NMOS is turned OFF (\(I_{DS} = 0\)). The output node charges toward \(V_{DD}\) through the enhancement load transistor.

Because the gate of the load transistor is connected to \(V_{DD}\), the load transistor operates in saturation and will conduct current until its source voltage reaches:

\[V_{\text{out}} = V_{DD} - V_t(V_{\text{out}})\]

Due to the body effect, the threshold voltage of the load transistor increases as the source voltage \(V_{\text{out}}\) rises above the substrate potential (\(0\text{ V}\)):

\[V_t(V_{\text{out}}) = V_{t0} + \gamma \left( \sqrt{|2\phi_F| + V_{\text{out}}} - \sqrt{|2\phi_F|} \right)\]

Assuming standard parameters (\(V_{t0} = 0.8\text{ V}\), \(\gamma = 0.5\text{ V}^{1/2}\), and surface potential \(|2\phi_F| = 0.6\text{ V}\)):

Solving this relationship iteratively:

  1. Let \(V_t \approx 0.8\text{ V}\) initially: \(V_{\text{out}} = 3.3 - 0.8 = 2.5\text{ V}\).
  2. Calculate threshold: \(V_t = 0.8 + 0.5\left(\sqrt{0.6 + 2.5} - \sqrt{0.6}\right) = 0.8 + 0.5(1.76 - 0.775) = 1.29\text{ V}\).
  3. Re-calculate output: \(V_{\text{out}} = 3.3 - 1.29 = 2.01\text{ V}\).
  4. Recalculate threshold: \(V_t = 0.8 + 0.5\left(\sqrt{0.6 + 2.01} - \sqrt{0.6}\right) = 0.8 + 0.5(1.615 - 0.775) = 1.22\text{ V}\).
  5. Re-calculate output: \(V_{\text{out}} = 3.3 - 1.22 = 2.08\text{ V}\).

Result: Under steady state, the output pulls up to \(V_{\text{out}} \approx 2.08\text{ V}\).

Module 4: Stick Diagrams & Layout Design

Stick diagram encoding color codes, design rules for wires and contacts, layouts of gates, and DRC/LVS verification.

💡 মনে রাখবে | Stick Diagrams & Layout Rules
  • Color Code Shortcut:
    • Green = Active (Diffusion) Layer (NMOS/PMOS)
    • Red = Polysilicon (Gate)
    • Blue = Metal 1 (Interconnections)
    • Yellow = p-well (CMOS substrate)
    • Black / X = Contact cut
  • Transistor Formation Rule: যখনই একটি লাল রেখা (Polysilicon) কোনো সবুজ রেখাকে (Diffusion) ক্রস করবে, তখনই সেখানে একটি ট্রানজিস্টর তৈরি হবে।
  • Demarcation Line: স্টিক ডায়াগ্রামে PMOS এবং NMOS জোনকে আলাদা করার জন্য একটি ড্যাশড্‌ কাল্পনিক রেখা টানা হয়। কোনো অ্যাক্টিভ লেয়ার (সবুজ) এটি ক্রস করতে পারে না, কেবল পলিসিলিকন (লাল) এবং মেটাল (নীল) এটি ক্রস করতে পারে।
  • Lambda (\(\lambda\)) Sizing: ডিজাইন রুল তৈরি করা হয় ল্যাম্বডা স্কেলে, যেখানে চ্যানেল লেন্থ \(L = 2\lambda\) এবং কন্টাক্ট সাইজ \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\)।
  • Layout Area Estimation Rule (ক্ষেত্রফল হিসাব): স্টিক ডায়াগ্রাম থেকে লেআউটের ক্ষেত্রফল হিসাব করতে বাউন্ডারি লাইনগুলোর দৈর্ঘ্যকে ল্যাম্বডা (\(\lambda\))-র গুণিতকে রূপান্তর করতে হবে। সাধারণ ইনভার্টারের সাইজ সাধারণত \(36\lambda^2\) থেকে \(48\lambda^2\) হয়ে থাকে।
  • Contact Rules (কন্টাক্ট কাটের নিয়ম): পলিসিলিকন থেকে ডিফিউশন বা মেটালে সরাসরি সংযোগ দেওয়া যায় না। এদের মধ্যে সংযোগের জন্য সর্বদা একটি কন্টাক্ট উইন্ডো (\(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\) সাইজের কালো বাক্স বা 'X') আঁকতে হয়। পলিসিলিকন ও ডিফিউশনের সরাসরি সংযোগকে Buried Contact বা Butting Contact বলে।

# Design Rules #

Design rules are the communication link between the designer specifying requirements and the fabricator who materializes them.

* Design rules are used to produce workable mask layouts from which the various layers in silicon will be formed or patterned.

Mainly two types:

a) lambda based design rules
b) Micron based design rules.

# Stick Diagram #

In MOS circuit has four basic layers.

  1. n-diffusion
  2. p-diffusion
  3. polysilicon
  4. Metal

Stick diagram may be used to convey layer information through the use of a color code.

Stick Diagram - some Rules

Rule 1: When two or more 'sticks' of the same type cross or touch each other that represents electrical contact

Fig: metal stick and polysilicon stick crossing (same type touching = contact)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : metal stick and polysilicon stick crossing       │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • (same type touching = contact)                         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Rule 2: When two or more sticks of different type cross or touch each other is no electrical contract.

Fig: different-type sticks crossing without contact (poly over diffusion, no dot) — note: "If electrical contract is needed we have to show the connection explicitly" (shown with a dot)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : different-type sticks crossing without contact   │
│         (poly over diffusion, no dot)                    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • note: "If electrical contract is needed we have to     │
│   show the connection explicitly" (shown with a dot)     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Rule 3: When a poly crosses diffusion it represent a transistor

Fig: poly crossing diffusion = transistor; if a contact dot is shown then it is not a transistor
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Device Structure / Schematic                     │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • poly crossing diffusion = transistor                   │
│ • if a contact dot is shown then it is not a transistor  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Rule 4: In cmos a demarcation line is drawn to avoid touching of p-diff with n-diff. All pmos must lie on one side of the line and all NMOS will have to be on the other side

+ Describe stick diagram rules (8)

Lambda-based design rules:

Lambda-based design rules in VLSI are a method to simplify the layout and fabrication of integrated circuits (ICs).

* The idea behind these rules is to make design rules technology

Page 74
📺 Watch Lecture Lambda Design Rules: Lambda-Based Design Rules for Mask Layout (EC Academy) ↗

page 74

-independent by scaling them with a single parameter, known as lambda (λ).

* Lambda represents half of the minimum feature size (like the smallest possible transistor gate width) for a specific fabrication process.

* line widths, separations and extensions are expressed in terms of λ.

* Minimum feature size is 2λ.

* Minimum feature size defined as the smallest scale at which a feature of a particular shape in a particular orientation can be manufactured.

# Design rules for wires (nmos and cmos)

Fig: Thinox design rules — Minimum width 2λ for n-diffusion and p-diffusion strips; Minimum separation 3λ (where specified); further strips at 2λ, 1λ, 2λ spacing; polysilicon strip at bottom (2λ width)

Explain Buried contact and Butting contact

Contact cuts

When making contacts between polysilicon and diffusion in nmos circuit it should be recognized that there are three possible approaches - poly to metal then metal to diffusion, buried contact poly to diffusion, a butting contact (poly to diff. using metal)

Double Metal MOS process Rules

The Double metal MOS process rule refers to the fabrication process that allows two separate metal layers to be used for interconnections on a single chip.

Process Rules:

  1. Minimum Metal width and spacing
    Defines the smallest width and spacing for metal traces to prevent short-circuiting or electromigration.
  2. Via Rules
    specifies the via size and spacing to connect metal layers while maintaining reliability.
  3. Metal layer interactions:
    Defines constraints on the overlap and alignment between the two metal layers and the underlying layers.

ch-7 # Stick Diagrams & Layout Design #

A stick diagram is a symbolic representation of a layout, used to convey information about the layer (metal, poly, diffusion, etc.) using color-coded or patterned lines, without showing exact dimensions.

# Purpose of Stick diagrams —

  1. To plan the layout / routing of a circuit before actual layout design.
  2. To estimate the area of the circuit.
  3. To capture the topology (relative placement and connections) of the circuit.

# Colour coding convention —

LayerColour / Representation
n-diffusionGreen
p-diffusionBrown/Yellow
PolysiliconRed
MetalBlue
Contact/ViaBlack dot / cross
Implant (p-well)Yellow dashed
Page 114

page 114

# Design Rules —

Design rules are a set of geometric constraints (minimum widths, spacings, overlaps) imposed on a layout to ensure that the fabricated circuit works correctly with acceptable yield, given the limitations of the fabrication process.

# Need for Design Rules —

  1. To ensure that devices/interconnects are correctly formed despite mask misalignment tolerances.
  2. To avoid unwanted shorts between different layers/devices.
  3. To maintain adequate yield of working chips.

# Types of Design rules —

  1. Lambda (λ) based rules — all dimensions are specified as multiples of λ, where λ = half of minimum feature size (channel length). Process independent, simple to use.
  2. Micron based rules — dimensions specified in absolute micron values, more accurate but process-dependent.
Page 115

page 115

# Lambda based Design rules (typical) —

RuleValue
Minimum poly width2λ
Minimum diffusion width3λ
Minimum poly-poly spacing2λ
Minimum diffusion-diffusion spacing3λ
Poly overlap of diffusion (gate)2λ
Diffusion overlap of poly (S/D)3λ
Minimum metal width3λ
Minimum metal-metal spacing3λ
Contact size\(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\)

# Stick diagram of CMOS Inverter —

Fig: CMOS Inverter stick diagram — \(V_{DD}\) (metal, top) with p-diffusion PMOS below, poly gate vertical strip through center forming Input, GND (metal, bottom) with n-diffusion NMOS above it, Output metal line from drain contacts of both

* Steps to draw stick diagram of CMOS inverter:

  1. Draw \(V_{DD}\) line (metal) at top and GND line (metal) at bottom.
  2. Draw p-diffusion strip near \(V_{DD}\) (for PMOS) and n-diffusion strip near GND (for NMOS).
  3. Draw a common poly line vertically crossing both diffusions — this forms the gates (input).
  4. Connect the diffusion to \(V_{DD}\)/GND with contacts (source), and connect drains of both transistors together with metal (output).

page 117

# Stick diagram of CMOS 2-input NAND gate —

Fig: 2-input NAND stick diagram — \(V_{DD}\) line top with two PMOS in parallel (p-diffusion), GND line bottom with two NMOS in series (n-diffusion), two poly gates (A, B) vertical, output taken from common node between PMOS drains and top NMOS drain

* In NAND gate: PMOS transistors connected in parallel (between output and \(V_{DD}\)), NMOS transistors connected in series (between output and GND).

Page 118

page 118

# Stick diagram of CMOS 2-input NOR gate —

Fig: 2-input NOR stick diagram — \(V_{DD}\) line top with two PMOS in series (p-diffusion), GND line bottom with two NMOS in parallel (n-diffusion), two poly gates (A, B) vertical, output from common node

* In NOR gate: PMOS transistors connected in series (between output and \(V_{DD}\)), NMOS transistors connected in parallel (between output and GND).

Note: NAND and NOR stick diagrams follow the duality rule — series in pull-up ⇄ parallel in pull-down and vice versa.

Page 119

page 119

ch-8 # MOS Layout / Layout design #

Layout is the actual geometric representation of an IC design showing the exact shapes, sizes, and positions of each layer (diffusion, poly, metal, contacts) as they will be manufactured on the mask.

# Difference between Stick diagram and Layout —

Stick DiagramLayout
Symbolic, not to scale.Drawn to exact scale/dimensions.
Used for planning topology quickly.Used as the final mask data for fabrication.
Design rules not strictly followed.Design rules strictly followed.
Fast to draw, less time consuming.Time consuming, needs CAD tools.
Page 120

page 120

# Layout Design Steps —

  1. Draw stick diagram (topology plan).
  2. Convert stick diagram to actual layout following λ-based design rules.
  3. Verify layout using DRC (Design Rule Check).
  4. Extract circuit from layout & compare with schematic — LVS (Layout Vs Schematic) check.
  5. Perform parasitic extraction & post-layout simulation.

# DRC (Design Rule Check) —

* DRC verifies that the layout satisfies all the minimum width/spacing/overlap rules specified by the fabrication process, to ensure manufacturability.

# LVS (Layout Versus Schematic) —

* LVS extracts the netlist (transistors & connections) from the layout and compares it against the original circuit schematic, to verify that the layout correctly implements the intended circuit.

Page 26

# Design rule checkers (DRC)

The wise designer will check for errors at all stages of the design, namely

  1. At the pencil and paper stage of the design of leaf-cells
  2. At the leaf-cells level once the layout is complete (e.g. when the CIF code for that leaf-cell has been generated)
  3. At the subsystem level to check that butting together and wiring up of leaf-cells is correctly done.
  4. Once the entire system layout has been completed.

# Test and Testability:

Three factors conspire to create considerable difficulties for the test engineer and indeed, for the designer testing his or her own prototype

  1. The sheer complexity of VLSI system
  2. The fact that the entire surface of the chip, other than over the pads, is sealed by an overglass layer and, thus, circuit nodes cannot be probed for monitoring or excitation
  3. With minor exceptions, there is no way that the circuit can be modified during test make it work.

It is also essential for faults to be detected as early as possible in the manufacture of a system.

Design Rules Applied:
1. Minimum Spacing: Poly to Diffusion = 1λ
2. Gate Formation: Poly crossing Diffusion = Transistor
3. Gate Extension: Poly must extend at least 2λ past active area boundary.

Page S-6 (Supp)

Layout Matching and Process Variation Design Rules

1. The Need for Matching in VLSI:

Due to process variations (like gradients in oxide thickness, doping concentration, or etching speed across a silicon wafer), two identically designed transistors can have different electrical properties (threshold voltage \(V_{th}\) mismatch or dimension mismatch \(\Delta W, \Delta L\)). In analog circuits (like current mirrors, differential pairs) and digital timing paths, layout matching techniques are critical to minimize these variations.

2. Matching Techniques & Layout Rules:

  • Common Centroid Layout: Devices are split and arranged symmetrically around a central point. This cancels out 1st-order linear gradients across the wafer. For two matching transistors A and B, they are split into halves and arranged in a 2x2 grid:
    \[\begin{pmatrix} A_1 & B_1 \\ B_2 & A_2 \end{pmatrix}\]
    Refer to common centroid diagram below:
  • Interdigitation: Interleaving fingers of matching transistors (e.g. ABBA or ABAB) to average out spatial variations.
  • Dummy Devices: Placing non-functional dummy transistors at the outer edges of the active array to shield matching devices from non-uniform edge etching and lithographic boundary effects.
  • Uniform Orientation: Ensuring matching devices have identical orientation and current flow direction (i.e. parallel gates) to minimize crystal orientation mismatch.
2024 2024)3)a [6 Marks] Draw the CMOS compound gate and also Stick Diagram of circuit for the function \(Y = \overline{(A + B + C) \cdot D}\).
▼
Sol

1. Pull-Down Network (PDN — NMOS) & Pull-Up Network (PUN — PMOS) Synthesis:

Given the inverted compound Boolean function \(Y = \overline{(A + B + C) \cdot D}\):

  • NMOS Pull-Down Network (PDN): Directly implements the uncomplemented expression \((A + B + C) \cdot D\):
    • The OR term \((A + B + C)\) is implemented by placing three NMOS transistors (\(M_{nA}, M_{nB}, M_{nC}\)) in parallel.
    • The AND operation \((\dots) \cdot D\) is implemented by placing NMOS transistor \(M_{nD}\) in series with the parallel combination of \(A, B, C\) between the output node \(Y\) and \(\text{GND}\).
  • PMOS Pull-Up Network (PUN): By CMOS duality, series and parallel connections are interchanged:
    • Three PMOS transistors (\(M_{pA}, M_{pB}, M_{pC}\)) are connected in series.
    • PMOS transistor \(M_{pD}\) is connected in parallel with the series branch of \((A, B, C)\) between \(V_{DD}\) and the output node \(Y\).
  • Euler Path & Stick Diagram Ordering: Using a consistent polysilicon gate ordering of \(A \to B \to C \to D\) allows continuous diffusion strips in both the \(p\)-diffusion and \(n\)-diffusion regions.
2024 2024)3)b [5 Marks] Explain the lambda (\(\lambda\)) based design rules with color layout and contact layer.
▼
Sol

1. Concept of Mead–Conway Lambda (\(\lambda\)) Design Rules:

Lambda (\(\lambda\))-based design rules express all geometrical layout constraints (minimum widths, minimum spacings, extensions, and overlaps) as integer multiples of a single fundamental length unit \(\lambda\), defined as half of the minimum feature size (channel length) (\(L_{\min} = 2\lambda\)). This makes mask layouts portable across different fabrication technology nodes simply by rescaling \(\lambda\).

2. Standard Stick & Mask Color Encoding and Width/Spacing Rules:

Layer NameStandard ColorMin. WidthMin. SpacingFunction in IC
n-Diffusion (Thinox)Green\(2\lambda\) (or \(3\lambda\))\(3\lambda\)Active source/drain/channel for NMOS
p-Diffusion (Thinox)Yellow\(2\lambda\) (or \(3\lambda\))\(3\lambda\)Active source/drain/channel for PMOS
PolysiliconRed\(2\lambda\)\(2\lambda\) (\(1\lambda\) to diff)Gate electrodes & local interconnects
Metal 1Blue\(3\lambda\)\(3\lambda\)Primary power (\(V_{DD}\)/GND) & signal routing
Metal 2Purple / Dark Blue\(4\lambda\)\(4\lambda\)Upper-level global bus routing
Implant / p-Well Dashed Yellow\(4\lambda\)\(5\lambda\) (\(3\lambda\) overlap)Depletion implant or CMOS well boundary
Contact Cut / ViaBlack Square / Cross (X)\(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\)\(2\lambda\)Connects Metal to Poly or Diffusion

3. Contact Layer & Transistor Rules:

  • Gate Overhang Rule: Where Red (Polysilicon) crosses Green/Yellow (Diffusion), a MOSFET channel is formed; Polysilicon must extend at least \(2\lambda\) beyond the diffusion edge.
  • Metal-to-Poly/Diffusion Contact Cut: Requires a \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\) contact cut centered inside a \(4\lambda \times 4\lambda\) overlap area (\(1\lambda\) surround on all sides by both layers).
  • Buried & Butting Contacts: Used to join Polysilicon and Diffusion directly without long Metal-1 jumpers.
2024 2024)3)c [3 Marks] What is the purpose of Butting contact in CMOS design.
▼
Sol

1. Purpose of a Butting Contact:

In MOS/CMOS layouts, Polysilicon (gate layer) and Diffusion (source/drain active layer) cannot be connected directly by crossing each other because their intersection automatically forms a parasitic MOSFET channel! A Butting Contact provides a compact, direct electrical connection between a Polysilicon line and a Diffusion region (such as tying the gate of a load transistor to its source, or cross-coupling gates and drains in SRAM/latches) while saving significant silicon area compared to separate contact cuts joined by a Metal runner.

2. Physical Structure & \(\lambda\) Design Rules:

  • The Polysilicon strip and Diffusion strip are brought together so that they overlap slightly (\(1\lambda\) overlap) at their abutting edge.
  • A \(2\lambda \times 4\lambda\) (or two adjacent \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\)) contact window is etched across the boundary exposing both Polysilicon and Diffusion.
  • A short \(4\lambda \times 6\lambda\) Metal-1 cap (strap) is deposited over the contact cut, electrically shorting the Polysilicon and Diffusion layers together with low contact resistance.
2024 2024)5)d [3 Marks] Draw the circuit diagram and stick diagram of 2 input NAND gate using NMOS and CMOS inverter.
▼
Sol

1. 2-Input Depletion-Load NMOS NAND Gate (\(Y = \overline{A \cdot B}\)):

  • Circuit Structure: Consists of one depletion-mode NMOS pull-up transistor (gate tied to source) and two enhancement-mode NMOS pull-down transistors (\(A\) and \(B\)) connected in series between output \(Y\) and \(\text{GND}\).
  • Ratio Sizing Rule: To maintain the overall inverter pull-up to pull-down ratio \(Z_{pu} / Z_{pd,\text{total}} = 4:1\) when both series drivers conduct, if \(Z_{pu} = 4\), each series NMOS transistor must have \(Z_{pd} = 1/2\) (or if each driver has \(Z_{pd} = 1\), the pull-up must have \(Z_{pu} = 8\)).

2. 2-Input Complementary CMOS NAND Gate (\(Y = \overline{A \cdot B}\)):

  • Circuit Structure: Features two PMOS transistors in parallel in the Pull-Up Network (between \(V_{DD}\) and \(Y\)) and two NMOS transistors in series in the Pull-Down Network (between \(Y\) and \(\text{GND}\)).
  • Operation: Output \(Y = 0\) only when both \(A = 1\) and \(B = 1\) (both series NMOS ON, both parallel PMOS OFF). For any other input combination, at least one PMOS is ON and pulls \(Y = V_{DD}\).
2021-22 2021-22)2)c [5 Marks] Draw the layout of a 2-input CMOS nand gate according to the design rules.
▼
Sol

1. Topological Structure & Design Rules Applied:

A 2-input static CMOS NAND gate (\(Y = \overline{A \cdot B}\)) consists of two PMOS transistors (\(M_{pA}, M_{pB}\)) connected in parallel inside an \(n\)-well at the top, and two NMOS transistors (\(M_{nA}, M_{nB}\)) connected in series on the \(p\)-substrate at the bottom:

  • Polysilicon Gates (Red): Two vertical Polysilicon strips for inputs \(A\) and \(B\) with minimum width \(= 2\lambda\), minimum Poly-to-Poly spacing \(= 2\lambda\), and gate overhang beyond diffusion \(\ge 2\lambda\).
  • PMOS Parallel Layout (\(p\)-Diffusion, Yellow): Shares a central drain contact connected to output \(Y\), while both outer source regions connect to the \(V_{DD}\) Metal-1 rail via \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\) contact cuts.
  • NMOS Series Layout (\(n\)-Diffusion, Green): Forms a single continuous horizontal \(n\)-diffusion strip crossed by both gates \(A\) and \(B\) with no intermediate contact needed between the two series channels! The left source connects to \(\text{GND}\) and the right drain connects to output \(Y\).
  • Metal-1 Rails (Blue): Minimum width \(= 3\lambda\) (typically \(4\lambda\) for power rails), Metal-to-Metal spacing \(\ge 3\lambda\).

Module 5: Basic Circuit Concepts & Delay

Sheet resistance, area capacitance calculations, the delay unit (tau), inverter delays, rise/fall time, power dissipation, and buffer chains.

💡 মনে রাখবে | Sheet Resistance & Delay Calculations
  • Sheet Resistance (\(R_s\)): যেকোনো আয়তকার মেটাল স্ট্রিপের রেজিস্ট্যান্স \(R = R_s \times (\frac{Length}{Width})\)। এখানে রেজিস্ট্যান্স শুধুমাত্র জ্যামিতিক অনুপাতের উপর নির্ভর করে, ক্ষেত্রফলের সাইজের উপর নয়।
  • Standard Gate Capacitance (\(\square C_g\)): একটি মিনিমাম সাইজ ট্রানজিস্টরের গেট ক্যাপাসিট্যান্সকে একক ধরা হয়।
  • Inverter Delay \(\tau\): ১টি মিনিমাম সাইজ NMOS ইনভার্টার তার নিজের ইনপুট ক্যাপাসিট্যান্স চার্জ করতে যে সময় নেয় তাকে \(\tau\) বলে (\(1\tau = R_s \times C_g \approx 0.1 - 0.3\text{ ns}\))।
  • CMOS Inverter Sizing: সমান রাইজ এবং ফল টাইম পাওয়ার জন্য PMOS-এর প্রস্থ NMOS-এর চেয়ে ২ থেকে ৩ গুণ বেশি করতে হবে (\(W_p = 2.5 W_n\)), কারণ গর্তের (hole) মোবিলিটি ইলেকট্রনের মোবিলিটির চেয়ে কম।
  • Super Buffer Cascade: বড় ক্যাপাসিティブ লোড ড্রাইভ করার জন্য অপটিমাল স্কেলিং রেশিও \(\alpha = e \approx 2.7\) এবং মোট স্টেজ সংখ্যা \(N = \ln(C_L / C_g)\)।
  • Tau (\(\tau\)) Calculation Shortcut: কোনো নোডের ক্যাপাসিট্যান্স \(C\) এবং ড্রাইভিং রেজিস্ট্যান্স \(R\) হলে নোডের ডিলে \(t_d \approx R \times C\)। একে RC delay বলা হয়। যদি একাধিক স্টেজ সিরিজে থাকে, তবে এলমোর ডিলে (Elmore Delay) সূত্র ব্যবহার করা হয়: \(T_D = \sum R_i C_i\)।
  • Rise/Fall Time (\(t_r / t_f\)) Estimations: NMOS-এর জন্য \(t_f \approx 3\tau\) থেকে \(4\tau\) and \(t_r \approx 8\tau\) (যদি পুল-আপ ডিভাইসটি ৮:১ ডিপ্লেশন মোড হয়)। CMOS-এর জন্য \(t_r \approx t_f\) নিশ্চিত করতে হলে \(W_p \approx 2.5 W_n\) রাখতে হয়।

ch-4 # Basic Circuit Concepts #

Sheet resistance

Sheet resistance is a measure of resistance for thin films, which are commonly used in semiconductor device. It represent the resistance of a square of material with uniform thickness.

Page 83
📺 Watch Lecture Sheet Resistance Concept: MOS Layers & Sheet Resistance Concept (Rs) (Education 4u) ↗

page 83

Fig: Resistive slab — length L, width W, thickness t, resistivity ρ, cross-section area A
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Resistive slab                                   │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • length L, width W, thickness t, resistivity ρ, cross-  │
│   section area A                                         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

where, ρ = Resistivity
W = Width
t = thickness
L = length

Consider the resistance RAB between two opposite faces.

\[R_{AB} = \frac{\rho L}{A}\text{ }\Omega \qquad (\text{where } A = \text{cross-section area} = W \cdot t)\]
\[= \frac{\rho L}{W \cdot t} \qquad \text{--- (1)}\]

Consider the case in which L=W, that is, a square of resistive material,

\[R_{AB} = \frac{\rho}{t} = R_s\]

Where, \(R_s\) = ohm per square or sheet resistance

\[= \frac{\rho}{t}\]

Note: * \(R_s\) is completely independent of the area of the square

Fig: Resistance calculation for transistor channel

For Fig(a),

\[\therefore \text{Channel resistance, } R = 1\text{ }\square \times R_s\text{ }\Omega/\square = 10^4\text{ }\Omega\]
\[\therefore Z = \frac{L}{W} = \frac{2\lambda}{2\lambda} = 1:1\]
Page 85
📺 Watch Lecture Sheet Resistance Concept: MOS Layers & Sheet Resistance Concept (Rs) (Education 4u) ↗

page 85

For Fig(b), Channel length, L=8λ and width W=2λ

\[\therefore Z = \frac{L}{W} = \frac{8\lambda}{2\lambda} = 4\]
\[\therefore \text{Channel resistance, } R = Z \cdot R_s = 4 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega\]

# This channel can be regarded as four \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\) square in series, thus giving a resistance of 4Rs.

Inverter Resistance calculation:

Ex- nmos

Fig: nmos inverter — \(V_{DD}\), \(L_{pu}:W_{pu}\) = 8λ:2λ (4:1), out, \(L_{pd}:W_{pd}\) = 2λ:2λ (1:1), \(V_{SS}\)(GND)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : nmos inverter                                    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • VDD, Lpu:Wpu = 8λ:2λ (4:1), out, Lpd:Wpd = 2λ:2λ       │
│   (1:1), Vss(GND)                                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

For pull-up-

\[Z_{pu} = \frac{L_{pu}}{W_{pu}} = 4\]
\[\therefore \text{ON resistance, } R_{\text{on}} = Z_{pu} \cdot R_{sn}\]
\[= 4 \times 10\text{ k}\Omega = 40\text{ k}\Omega\]

Here, \(R_{sn} = \text{n-channel sheet resistance} = 10\text{ k}\Omega\)
For pull-up, \(Z_{pu} = 4:1\)

Page 86
📺 Watch Lecture Sheet Resistance Concept: MOS Layers & Sheet Resistance Concept (Rs) (Education 4u) ↗

page 86

For pull-down ratio,

\[Z_{p.d} = 1\]
\[\text{ON resistance, } R_{\text{on}} = Z_{p.d} \cdot R_{sn}\]
\[= 1 \times 10\text{ k}\Omega = 10\text{ k}\Omega\]

\(\therefore\) Pull-up : Pull-down ratio \(= Z_{pu} : Z_{pd} = 4:1\) for nMOS, and \(Z_{pu} : Z_{pd} = 1:1\) for CMOS

In this case, On resistance (\(V_{DD}\) to GND)

\[= 40\text{ k}\Omega + 10\text{ k}\Omega = 50\text{ k}\Omega\]

Ex- cmos

Fig: cmos inverter — \(V_{DD}\), \(L_{pu}:W_{pu}\)=1:1, \(V_{\text{in}}\), \(V_{\text{out}}\), \(L_{pd}:W_{pd}\)=1:1, \(V_{SS}\)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : cmos inverter                                    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • VDD, Lpu:Wpu=1:1, Vin, Vout, Lpd:Wpd=1:1, Vss          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

For pull-up,

\[Z_{pu} = 1\]
\[\text{ON resistance, } R_{\text{on}} = Z_{pu} \cdot R_{sp}\]
\[= 1 \times 25\text{ k}\Omega = 25\text{ k}\Omega\]

\(R_s = 2.5 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega = 25\text{ k}\Omega\)

For pull-down,

\[Z_{pd} = 1\]
\[\text{ON resistance, } R_{\text{on}} = Z_{pd} \cdot R_{sn}\]
\[= 1 \times 10\text{ k}\Omega = 10\text{ k}\Omega\]

Here, \(R_{sn} = 10\text{ k}\Omega\)

* A ratio rule does not apply and there is no static resistance from \(V_{DD}\) to \(V_{SS}\).

# Silicides:

Silicides compound formed by combining silicon with metal. They are widely used in semiconductor manufacturing to create low-resistance contract and interconnections, which are essential for improving performance and speed of integrated circuits.

Purpose of silicides-

  1. low resistance contacts:
    Silicides like titanium silicide (TiSi2), cobalt silicides (CoSi2) are used to reduced contract resistance between metal and silicon.
  2. Ohmic Contract
    Silicides help create stable, ohmic contacts, ensuring good electrically conductivity.

Area Capacitance of layer:

Area capacitance is formed among the metal interconnects and base layer.

Page 88
📺 Watch Lecture Area Capacitance: Area Capacitance Calculations & Standard Unit Cg (LEARN AND GROW) ↗

page 88

\[C = \frac{\varepsilon_0 \varepsilon_{\text{ins}} A}{D}\text{ Farads}\]

Where, D = thickness of silicon dioxide
A = area of plates
\(\varepsilon_{\text{ins}} = \text{relative permittivity of SiO}_2 = 4.0\)

\(\varepsilon_0 = 8.85 \times 10^{-14}\text{ F/cm}\) (permittivity of free space)

\[\therefore \text{Capacitance per unit area} = \left(\text{pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\right)\]

# Standard unit of capacitance \(\square C_g\)

standard unit of capacitance denote by \(\square C_g\) define gate to channel capacitance of mos transistor W=L=feature size.

Page 89
📺 Watch Lecture Area Capacitance: Area Capacitance Calculations & Standard Unit Cg (LEARN AND GROW) ↗

page 89

For example-

For 5μm mos circuits:

\[\text{Standard square area} = 5\,\mu\text{m} \times 5\,\mu\text{m} = 25\,\mu\text{m}^2 \quad (\text{minimum-size transistor})\]
\[\text{Unit capacitance (from table)} = 4 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\]

Thus, standard value \(\square C_g = 25\,\mu\text{m}^2 \times 4 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\)

\[= 0.01\text{ pF}\]

For 2μm mos circuit (orbit):

\[\text{Standard square area} = 2\,\mu\text{m} \times 2\,\mu\text{m} = 4\,\mu\text{m}^2\]
\[\text{Gate capacitance value} = 8 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\]

Thus, standard value \(\square C_g = 4\,\mu\text{m}^2 \times 8 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\)

\[= 0.0032\text{ pF}\]

For 1.2μm mos circuits (orbit):

\[\text{Standard square area} = 1.2\,\mu\text{m} \times 1.2\,\mu\text{m} = 1.44\,\mu\text{m}^2\]
\[\text{Gate capacitance value} = 16 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\]

\(\therefore\) Thus, standard value \(\square C_g = 1.44\,\mu\text{m}^2 \times 16 \times 10^{-4}\text{ pF}/\mu\text{m}^2\)

\[= 0.0023\text{ pF}\]

# The delay unit (τ) #

A delay unit represent the time taken for a signal to propagate through a specific circuit element or path.

If we consider the case of one standard (feature size square) gate area capacitance being changed through one feature size square of n channel resistance.

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Model for derivation of τ                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
\[\therefore \text{Time constant } \tau = \left(1\,R_{s(\text{n-channel})} \times 1\,\square C_g\right)\text{ seconds}\]

For 5μm technology,

\[\tau = 10^4\text{ }\Omega \times 0.01\text{ pF} = 0.1\text{ ns}\]

For 2μm (orbit) technology,

\[\tau = 2 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega \times 0.0032\text{ pF}\]
\[= 0.064\text{ ns}\]

For 1.2μm (orbit) technology,

\[\tau = 2 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega \times 0.0023\text{ pF}\]
\[= 0.046\text{ ns}\]

τ = 0.2 to 0.3nsec is a typical design figure.

\[\text{Transit time, } \tau_{sd} = \frac{L^2}{\mu_n V_{DS}}\]

📺 Watch Lecture The Delay Unit Tau: The Delay Unit (tau) & Propagation Delay (E KRISH) ↗

page 95

Note:

* \(V_{DS}\) varies as \(C_g\) changes from 0volts to 63% of \(V_{DD}\) in period τ.

Ex- \(V_{DS}\) is the average value = 3 volts

For 5μm technology,

\[\tau_d = \frac{L^2}{\mu_n \cdot V_{DS}}\]

Here, \(L = 5\,\mu\text{m},\; \mu_n = 650\text{ cm}^2/\text{V}\cdot\text{s},\; V_{DS} = 3\text{ V}\)

\[= \frac{(5\,\mu\text{m})^2}{650\text{ cm}^2/\text{V}\cdot\text{s} \times 3\text{ V}} \times \frac{10^9\text{ ns}\cdot\text{cm}^2}{10^8\,\mu\text{m}^2\cdot\text{s}}\]
\[= 0.13\text{ ns}\]

# Inverter Delays #

Inverter delay refers to the time it takes for a signal to propagate through an inverter.

Two main component-

  1. Rise time -
    The time from when the input goes low(0) to when the output rise to high(1).
  2. Fall time -
    The time (delay) from when the input goes high(1) to when the output falls to low(0).

in MOS inverter:

# Rise-time estimation (\(\tau_r\))

In this analysis we assume that the p-device stays in saturation for the entire changing period of the load capacitor \(C_L\).

Fig: Rise-time model — \(V_{DD}\) → p-device (\(I_{DSp}\) current) → node with switch (\(V_{\text{in}}\)=\(V_{GS}\), 1/0 states) → \(C_L\), \(V_{\text{out}}\) to \(V_{SS}\)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : Rise-time model                                  │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • VDD → p-device (Idsp current) → node with switch       │
│   (Vin=Vgs, 1/0 states) → CL, Vout to Vss                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Page 98

page 98

The saturation current for the p-transistor is given by

\[I_{DSp} = \frac{\beta_p}{2}\left(V_{GS} - |V_{tp}|\right)^2 \qquad \text{--- (1)}\]

This current changes Q and since if magnitude is approximately constant,

\[V_{\text{out}} = \frac{I_{DSp} \cdot t}{C_L}\]
\[t = \frac{2\,C_L \cdot V_{\text{out}}}{\beta_p\left(V_{GS} - |V_{tp}|\right)^2}\]

We now assume that \(t = \tau_r\) when \(V_{\text{out}} = +V_{DD}\),

\[\tau_r = \frac{2\,V_{DD} \cdot C_L}{\beta_p\left(V_{DD} - |V_{tp}|\right)^2}\]

With \(|V_{tp}| = 0.2\,V_{DD}\) then,

\[\tau_r \approx \frac{3\,C_L}{\beta_p \cdot V_{DD}}\]

Fall-time (\(\tau_f\))

* n-transistor

Requirements in contemplating large VLSI system:

  1. CAD: The VLSI designer will need computer-aided design assistance, not only to assist in the design but also to handle the sheer complexity of the information needed to express the physical aspect of the design in a form suitable for translation into silicon.
  2. Verification tools are essential to verify that the design is physically and logically connect and will perform correctly at the desired speed.
  3. Testability. The designer must, from the outset, face up to the requirement of being able to test a system once it is realized in silicon
  4. Test facilities. Not only must testability be designed in but complex system will need sophisticated equipment to actually test for connect operation.
Page S-1 (Supp)

Buffer Chain Design to Drive Large Capacitive Loads

The Problem of Large Capacitive Loads:

When a minimum-sized inverter is used to drive a large capacitive load \(C_L\) (such as long metal bus lines or off-chip pads), the propagation delay becomes extremely large. This is because the driving current \(I\) of the minimum-sized transistors is small, and the charging time \(t = C_L V / I\) increases linearly with \(C_L\).

The Buffer Chain Solution:

To drive a large capacitive load efficiently, we use a cascaded chain of \(N\) inverters where each stage is scaled up in size by a factor of \(\alpha\) relative to the preceding stage. The scaling ratio is defined as:

\[\alpha = \frac{W_{i+1}}{W_i}\]

If the input capacitance of the first minimum-sized inverter is \(C_g\), then the input capacitance of the second stage is \(\alpha C_g\), the third is \(\alpha^2 C_g\), and so on. The final load capacitance is matched to the output of the last stage:

\[Y = \frac{C_L}{C_g} = \alpha^N\]

Derivation of Optimal Scaling Factor (\(\alpha\)):

The delay of each stage is proportional to its sizing ratio \(\alpha\). For \(N\) stages, the total delay is:

\[t_d = N \cdot \alpha \cdot \tau\]

From the capacitance ratio, the number of stages is:

\[N = \frac{\ln(Y)}{\ln(\alpha)}\]

Substituting \(N\) into the total delay equation gives:

\[t_d = \ln(Y) \cdot \frac{\alpha}{\ln(\alpha)} \cdot \tau\]

To find the value of \(\alpha\) that minimizes delay, we differentiate the term \(\frac{\alpha}{\ln(\alpha)}\) with respect to \(\alpha\) and set it to zero:

\[\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\alpha}{\ln(\alpha)}\right) = \frac{\ln(\alpha) - 1}{(\ln(\alpha))^2} = 0 \implies \ln(\alpha) = 1 \implies \alpha = e \approx 2.718\]

Thus, the mathematically optimal scaling factor is \(\alpha = e \approx 2.7\), and the optimal number of stages is \(N = \ln(Y)\).

Practical Sizing in VLSI:

In practice, scaling factors of \(\alpha = 3\) or \(\alpha = 4\) are used to reduce layout area and the number of stages, resulting in a negligible increase in delay (less than 4% from optimal).

Fig: Cascaded Buffer Chain for High Capacitive Load

CMOS Inverter Sizing and Power Consumption Estimation

1. Design for Equal Rise and Fall Times:

To achieve equal rise time (\(t_r\)) and fall time (\(t_f\)), the drive strength of the pull-up PMOS must equal that of the pull-down NMOS. Since the mobility of electrons (\(\mu_n\)) is approximately 2.5 times higher than that of holes (\(\mu_p\)) in silicon, we must size the width of the PMOS (\(W_p\)) relative to the NMOS (\(W_n\)) as follows:

\[\beta_n = \beta_p \implies \mu_n C_{ox} \frac{W_n}{L_n} = \mu_p C_{ox} \frac{W_p}{L_p}\]

Assuming identical channel lengths (\(L_n = L_p\)):

\[W_p \approx 2.5 \cdot W_n\]

This balances the pull-up and pull-down currents, yielding symmetric transfer characteristics and equal propagation delay.

2. Power Consumption Estimation in CMOS:

Total power dissipation is the sum of three components:

  1. Dynamic Power (\(P_{dynamic}\)): Caused by charging and discharging of the load capacitance \(C_L\) at switching frequency \(f\):
    \[P_{dynamic} = \alpha \cdot C_L \cdot V_{DD}^2 \cdot f\]
    where \(\alpha\) is the switching activity factor.
  2. Static Power (\(P_{static}\)): Caused by leakage currents (subthreshold leakage, gate oxide leakage) flowing when the circuit is in steady state:
    \[P_{static} = I_{leakage} \cdot V_{DD}\]
  3. Short-circuit Power (\(P_{sc}\)): Direct-path current flowing from \(V_{DD}\) to GND when both PMOS and NMOS are transiently ON during output transitions.
2024 2024)4)a [4 Marks] Determine the channel resistance of a nMOS having length \(L = 8\lambda\) and width \(W = 2\lambda\), where \(R_s = 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\).
▼
Sol

1. Formula & Concept of Sheet Resistance:

The resistance \(R\) of a uniform rectangular conducting channel of length \(L\), width \(W\), thickness \(t\), and resistivity \(\rho\) is given by:

\[R = \frac{\rho L}{W \cdot t} = R_s \times \left(\frac{L}{W}\right) = Z \cdot R_s\]

where \(R_s = \frac{\rho}{t}\) is the sheet resistance in \(\Omega/\square\) (ohms per square) and \(Z = \frac{L}{W}\) is the number of unit squares in series along the current path.

2. Calculation:

  • Given channel length: \(L = 8\lambda\)
  • Given channel width: \(W = 2\lambda\)
  • Given n-channel sheet resistance: \(R_s = 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\)

First, find the length-to-width ratio (number of \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda\) squares in series):

\[Z = \frac{L}{W} = \frac{8\lambda}{2\lambda} = 4\text{ squares}\]

Now, multiply by the sheet resistance \(R_s\):

\[R = Z \cdot R_s = 4 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square = 4 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega = 40\text{ k}\Omega\]
2024 2024)4)b [4 Marks] Describe the working principle of nMOS and CMOS inverter pair delay.
▼
Sol

Because a single inverter experiences asymmetric rise (\(\tau_r\)) and fall (\(\tau_f\)) delays, overall propagation speed in VLSI is characterized using the Inverter Pair Delay (\(T_d\)) across two identical cascaded inverters where one stage pulls down while the next stage pulls up.

1. NMOS Inverter Pair Delay:

  • Consider a standard \(4:1\) depletion-load NMOS inverter where \(Z_{pu} = 4\) (\(R_{pu} = 4\,R_{sn} = 40\text{ k}\Omega\)) and \(Z_{pd} = 1\) (\(R_{pd} = 1\,R_{sn} = 10\text{ k}\Omega\)), driving a minimum-size gate load of \(C_g = 1\,\square C_g\).
  • Stage 1 (Fall Transition, \(1 \to 0\)): Pull-down NMOS discharges \(1\,\square C_g\) through \(R_{pd} = 1\,R_{sn}\), giving a delay of \(\tau_f = 1\,R_{sn} \times 1\,\square C_g = 1\tau\).
  • Stage 2 (Rise Transition, \(0 \to 1\)): Pull-up depletion load charges \(1\,\square C_g\) through \(R_{pu} = 4\,R_{sn}\), giving a delay of \(\tau_r = 4\,R_{sn} \times 1\,\square C_g = 4\tau\).
\[T_{d(\text{NMOS pair})} = \tau_f + \tau_r = \left(1 + \frac{Z_{pu}}{Z_{pd}}\right)\tau = (1 + 4)\tau = 5\tau\]

2. Minimum-Size CMOS Inverter Pair Delay:

  • In a \(1:1\) CMOS inverter, the input drives both the PMOS and NMOS gates, so the input load capacitance is doubled: \(C_{\text{in}} = 2\,\square C_g\).
  • Since hole mobility is lower than electron mobility, the PMOS channel sheet resistance is \(R_{sp} \approx 2.5\,R_{sn} = 25\text{ k}\Omega\) while NMOS is \(R_{sn} = 10\text{ k}\Omega\).
  • Stage 1 (Fall Delay): NMOS discharges \(2\,\square C_g\) through \(1\,R_{sn} \implies \tau_f = 1\,R_{sn} \times 2\,\square C_g = 2\tau\).
  • Stage 2 (Rise Delay): PMOS charges \(2\,\square C_g\) through \(2.5\,R_{sn} \implies \tau_r = 2.5\,R_{sn} \times 2\,\square C_g = 5\tau\).
\[T_{d(\text{CMOS pair})} = \tau_f + \tau_r = 2\tau + 5\tau = 7\tau\]
2024 2024)4)c [4 Marks] Calculate the area capacitance value from the given Fig below.
▼
Sol

We sum the capacitances of the individual layers relative to the substrate based on standard relative area capacitance values:

  • Metal Area: \(100\lambda \times 3\lambda = 300\lambda^2 = 75 \times (2\lambda \times 2\lambda)\).
    \(C_m = 75 \times 0.075\,\square C_g = 5.625\,\square C_g\) (or per unit \(\lambda^2\): \(300 \times 0.075 = 22.5\,\square C_g\)).
  • Gate Area: \(2\lambda \times 2\lambda = 4\lambda^2 = 1\text{ standard square}\).
    \(C_{\text{gate}} = 1 \times 1.0\,\square C_g = 1.0\,\square C_g\) (or \(4 \times 1.0 = 4.0\,\square C_g\)).
  • Diffusion Area (excluding channel): \((4\lambda \times 2\lambda) - 4\lambda^2 = 4\lambda^2\).
    \(C_{\text{diff}} = 1 \times 0.25\,\square C_g = 0.25\,\square C_g\) (or \(4 \times 0.25 = 1.0\,\square C_g\)).
\[C_{\text{total}} = C_m + C_{\text{gate}} + C_{\text{diff}} = 5.625 + 1.0 + 0.25 = 6.875\,\square C_g \quad (\text{or } 27.5\,\square C_g \text{ unnormalized})\]
2024 2024)4)d [2 Marks] What is sheet resistance and standard unit of capacitance.
▼
Sol

1. Sheet Resistance (\(R_s\)):

The electrical resistance between opposite edges of a uniform square slab of conducting or semiconducting IC layer (metal, polysilicon, or diffusion/channel) of resistivity \(\rho\) and uniform thickness \(t\). For a rectangular conductor of length \(L\) and width \(W\):

\[R = \frac{\rho \cdot L}{t \cdot W} = R_s \cdot \left(\frac{L}{W}\right) = Z \cdot R_s \qquad \text{where } R_s = \frac{\rho}{t}\text{ }(\Omega/\square)\]

When \(L = W\) (one unit square \(\square\)), \(R = R_s\), completely independent of the physical size of the square. Typical \(5\,\mu\text{m}\) MOS values: \(R_s(\text{n-channel}) \approx 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\), \(R_s(\text{p-channel}) \approx 2.5 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\), \(R_s(\text{Metal}) \approx 0.03\text{ }\Omega/\square\).

2. Standard Unit of Capacitance (\(\square C_g\)):

Defined as the gate-to-channel capacitance of a minimum-sized MOS transistor having unit gate length \(L = 2\lambda\) and unit width \(W = 2\lambda\) (standard gate area \(= 2\lambda \times 2\lambda = 4\lambda^2\)):

\[\square C_g = C_{\text{ox}} \times (2\lambda \times 2\lambda) = \frac{\varepsilon_{\text{ox}}}{t_{\text{ox}}} \cdot 4\lambda^2 \quad (\text{typically } \square C_g \approx 0.01\text{ pF in } 5\,\mu\text{m MOS})\]
2024 2024)5)a [3 Marks] What is ratioed and ratioless inverter.
▼
Sol

1. Ratioed Inverter:

A logic family in which the valid output low voltage level (\(V_{OL}\)) and noise margins depend directly on the geometric aspect ratio (\(Z_{pu} / Z_{pd}\)) of the pull-up load device to the pull-down driver transistor. When the input is HIGH (\(V_{\text{in}} = V_{DD}\)), both the pull-up load and pull-down driver conduct simultaneously, forming a resistive voltage divider between \(V_{DD}\) and \(\text{GND}\):

\[V_{OL} = V_{DD} \cdot \frac{R_{pd}}{R_{pu} + R_{pd}} < V_{\text{th}}\]

Examples: Resistive-load NMOS, Depletion-load NMOS (requires \(Z_{pu}/Z_{pd} \ge 4:1\) or \(8:1\)), and Pseudo-NMOS inverters.

2. Ratioless Inverter:

A logic family in which the steady-state output voltage levels (\(V_{OH} = V_{DD}\) and \(V_{OL} = 0\text{ V}\)) are completely independent of the relative transistor sizes (\(W/L\)). In steady state, one network is fully ON while the complementary network is fully OFF, eliminating any DC resistive voltage divider or static current path. Transistor sizing affects only the transient switching speed (\(\tau_r, \tau_f\)) and switching threshold \(V_{\text{inv}}\), not the logic levels.

Examples: Complementary Static CMOS inverter and Dynamic precharge-evaluate logic.

FeatureRatioed Inverter (e.g., Depletion NMOS, Pseudo-NMOS)Ratioless Inverter (e.g., Static CMOS)
Output Low (\(V_{OL}\))\(V_{OL} > 0\text{ V}\) (depends on \(Z_{pu}/Z_{pd}\))Full rail-to-rail (\(V_{OL} = 0\text{ V}, V_{OH} = V_{DD}\))
Static PowerHigh when output is logic 0 (DC current path exists)Virtually zero (\(P_{\text{static}} \approx 0\))
Transistor Count\(N + 1\) transistors for \(N\)-input gate\(2N\) transistors for \(N\)-input gate
2021-22 2021-22)4)a [4 Marks] What is the concept of sheet resistance and standard unit of capacitance \(\square C_g\)?
▼
Sol

1. Sheet Resistance (\(R_s\)):

A method to measure the resistance of thin conductive layers of uniform thickness (\(t\)) and resistivity (\(\rho\)). For a rectangular conductor block of length \(L\) and width \(W\), resistance is defined as:

\[R = \left(\frac{\rho}{t}\right) \times \left(\frac{L}{W}\right) = R_s \times \left(\frac{L}{W}\right)\]

Where \(R_s = \frac{\rho}{t}\) is the sheet resistance, expressed in ohms per square (\(\Omega/\square\)). The resistance of a square (\(L = W\)) is constant and equal to \(R_s\) regardless of its dimensions.

2. Standard Unit of Capacitance (\(\square C_g\)):

Represented as \(\square C_g\), it is the gate-to-channel capacitance of a minimum-sized MOS transistor (area \(= 2\lambda \times 2\lambda\)). It acts as a baseline unit to calculate capacitances of other layers (diffusion, poly, metal) by expressing them as relative fractions (e.g. metal is \(0.075\,\square C_g\), diffusion is \(0.25\,\square C_g\)).

2021-22 2021-22)4)b [5 Marks] An inverter PMOS has \(L_{pu}:W_{pu} = 1:1\), NMOS has \(L_{pd}:W_{pd} = 1:1\). Find the ratio impedance and total sheet resistance.
▼
Sol

Given parameters: n-transistor channel sheet resistance \(R_{s,n} = 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\), p-transistor channel sheet resistance \(R_{s,p} = 2.5 \times 10^4\text{ }\Omega/\square\).

For \(1:1\) aspect ratio devices (\(L/W = 1\)):

  • PMOS channel resistance (\(R_{pu}\)) \(= 2.5 \times 10^4 \times 1 = 25\text{ k}\Omega\).
  • NMOS channel resistance (\(R_{pd}\)) \(= 1.0 \times 10^4 \times 1 = 10\text{ k}\Omega\).

1. Ratio of Impedance (\(Z_{pu} / Z_{pd}\) and \(R_{pu} / R_{pd}\)):

\[\frac{Z_{pu}}{Z_{pd}} = \frac{1}{1} = 1:1 \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{R_{pu}}{R_{pd}} = \frac{25\text{ k}\Omega}{10\text{ k}\Omega} = 2.5 : 1\]

2. Total Sheet Resistance:

In transition, if we sum the sheet resistances of both channels in series:

\[R_{\text{total}} = R_{pu} + R_{pd} = 25\text{ k}\Omega + 10\text{ k}\Omega = 35\text{ k}\Omega\]

Module 6: Scaling of MOS Circuits

Constant field scaling and constant voltage scaling models, derived device parameters, and scaling comparison tables.

💡 মনে রাখবে | Scaling Models Quick Guide
  • Constant Field Scaling (\(1/\beta\)): ডাইমেনশন এবং ভোল্টেজ উভয়কেই সমান অনুপাতে কমানো হয়।
    • Gate Capacitance (\(C_g\)) স্কেল ডাউন হয় \(1/\beta\) হারে।
    • Transit Time (\(\tau_{sd}\)) স্কেল ডাউন হয় \(1/\beta\) হারে (দ্রুত কাজ করে)।
    • Frequency (\(f\)) বৃদ্ধি পায় \(\beta\) গুণ।
    • Power Dissipation (\(P\)) স্কেল ডাউন হয় \(1/\beta^2\) হারে (অত্যন্ত সাশ্রয়ী)।
    • পাওয়ার ডেনসিটির কোনো পরিবর্তন হয় না (স্থির থাকে)। এটি হট-ইলেকট্রন ইফেক্ট কমায়।
  • Constant Voltage Scaling (\(1/\beta\) dimension, \(1\) voltage): ডাইমেনশন কমানো হলেও ভোল্টেজ একই রাখা হয়।
    • ড্রেন কারেন্ট বাড়ে \(\beta\) গুণ।
    • পাওয়ার ডেনসিটির নাটকীয় বৃদ্ধি ঘটে (\(\beta^3\) গুণ), যা চিপের তাপমাত্রা অনেক বাড়িয়ে দেয়।
  • Hot Carrier Effect (হট ক্যারিয়ার ইফেক্ট): কনস্ট্যান্ট ভোল্টেজ স্কেলিং-এ ডাইমেনশন কমলেও ভোল্টেজ স্থির থাকার কারণে ইলেকট্রিক ফিল্ড অনেক বেড়ে যায়। এর ফলে ইলেকট্রনগুলো অতি উচ্চ গতিশক্তি লাভ করে গেট অক্সাইডে ঢুকে পড়ে ট্রানজিস্টর নষ্ট করে দেয়।
  • Shortcut Table: ফিজিক্যাল সাইজ সবসময় \(1/\beta\) স্কেলে ছোট হয়। কারেন্ট ডেনসিটি কনস্ট্যান্ট ফিল্ডে স্থির থাকে, কিন্তু কনস্ট্যান্ট ভোল্টেজে \(\beta\) গুণ বাড়ে।

page 99

ch-5 # Scaling of MOS Circuits #

Microelectronics technology may be characterised in terms of several indicators - (on adv.)

  1. Minimum feature size
  2. Number of gates on one chip.
  3. Power dissipation
  4. Maximum operational frequency
  5. Die size
  6. Production cost.

What is scaling in VLSI?

The reduction of the size ie the dimensions of MOSFETs, is commonly referred to as scaling.

* In order to meet the demand of high density chips in Mos technology it is required that MOSFET are scaled down ie reduction in the size of transistor, so that high packaging density can be achieved.

# Scaling Models And Scaling factors -

Fig: MOSFET 3D scaling structure — W/α, L/α, t/α, D/α dimensions marked with poly gate over n+ regions in P-type substrate, "Doping N" labeled
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ TECHNICAL SCHEMATIC SPECIFICATION                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Block : MOSFET 3D scaling structure                      │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ • W/α, L/α, t/α, D/α dimensions marked with poly gate    │
│   over n+ regions in P-type substrate, "Doping N"        │
│   labeled                                                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

* Two models used to describe scaling of MOS device parameters:

  1. Constant Field scaling.
  2. Constant voltage scaling.

# Constant field scaling —

* In this scaling model, the electric field is kept constant while the horizontal & vertical dimensions along with voltage are scaled with the same factor.

* Here, α = scaling factor, α>1.

ParameterRelationScaled value
Channel length LL\(L/\alpha\)
Channel width WW\(W/\alpha\)
Gate oxide thickness \(t_{\text{ox}}\)\(t_{\text{ox}}\)\(t_{\text{ox}}/\alpha\)
Supply voltage \(V_{DD}\)\(V_{DD}\)\(V_{DD}/\alpha\)
Doping density \(N_A\)\(N_A\)\(\alpha \cdot N_A\)
Page 100
📺 Watch Lecture Scaling Models & Factors (Part 1): MOS Circuit Scaling Models & Scaling Factors (Education 4u) ↗

page 100

# Derived / Device parameters under constant field scaling —

ParameterFormulaScaling
Gate area (\(A_g\))\(A_g = W \cdot L\)\(1/\alpha^2\)
Gate capacitance/area (\(C_o\))\(C_o = \varepsilon_{\text{ox}} / t_{\text{ox}}\)\(\alpha\)
Gate capacitance (\(C_g\))\(C_g = C_o \cdot A_g\)\(1/\alpha\)
Carrier density (\(Q_{\text{on}}\))\(Q_{\text{on}} = C_o \cdot V_{DD}\)1
Channel resistance (\(R_{\text{on}}\))\(R_{\text{on}} = \dfrac{L}{W \cdot \mu \cdot Q_{\text{on}}}\)1

* Since \(R_{\text{on}}\) does not change with scaling, delay of a gate is given by,

\[\tau = R_{\text{on}} \cdot C_g\]
\[\therefore \tau \text{ scales as } \frac{1}{\alpha}\]

So, under constant field scaling, device speed increases proportional to α.

# Power dissipation —

\[P_d = V_{DD} \cdot I_{\text{on}}\]

Since \(I_{\text{on}}\) scales as \(1/\alpha\) and \(V_{DD}\) scales as \(1/\alpha\),

\[P_d \text{ scales as } \frac{1}{\alpha^2}\]

* Power density (Power/Area):

\[\frac{P_d}{A_g} = \frac{1/\alpha^2}{1/\alpha^2} = 1\]

Power density remains constant under constant field scaling — this is the major advantage of this model.

Page 101
📺 Watch Lecture Device Parameters Scaling (Part 2): Transistor Device Parameters Under Scaling (Education 4u) ↗

page 101

# Constant Voltage Scaling —

* In this scaling model, the horizontal and vertical dimensions are scaled by a factor α, but the supply voltage \(V_{DD}\) and the terminal voltages are kept constant.

* This model is preferred practically since it maintains compatibility of voltage levels with older circuits/systems (TTL compatibility).

ParameterRelationScaled value
Channel length LL\(L/\alpha\)
Channel width WW\(W/\alpha\)
Gate oxide thickness \(t_{\text{ox}}\)\(t_{\text{ox}}\)\(t_{\text{ox}}/\alpha\)
Supply voltage \(V_{DD}\)\(V_{DD}\)\(V_{DD}\) (unchanged)
Doping density \(N_A\)\(N_A\)\(\alpha^2 \cdot N_A\)

# Derived parameters —

Gate area (\(A_g\))\(1/\alpha^2\)
Gate capacitance/area (\(C_o\))\(\alpha\)
Gate capacitance (\(C_g\))\(1/\alpha\)
Carrier density (\(Q_{\text{on}}\))\(\alpha\)
Drain current (\(I_{\text{on}}\))\(\alpha\)
Page 102
📺 Watch Lecture Device Parameters Scaling (Part 2): Transistor Device Parameters Under Scaling (Education 4u) ↗
📺 Watch Lecture Moore's Law & Scaling Limits: Moore's Law & Limits of Silicon Scaling (2 Minute Expert) ↗

page 102

* Delay:

\[\tau = \frac{C_g \cdot V_{DD}}{I_{\text{on}}}\]
\[\tau \text{ scales as } \frac{1/\alpha}{\alpha} = \frac{1}{\alpha^2}\]

So under constant voltage scaling, delay reduces faster (by \(1/\alpha^2\)) — device speed is higher than constant field scaling.

* Power dissipation:

\[P_d = V_{DD} \cdot I_{\text{on}}\]

Since \(V_{DD}\) is constant and \(I_{\text{on}}\) scales as α,

\[P_d \text{ scales as } \alpha\]

* Power density:

\[\frac{P_d}{A_g} = \frac{\alpha}{1/\alpha^2} = \alpha^3\]

Power density increases drastically (\(\alpha^3\)) — this is the major drawback of constant voltage scaling, leading to severe heating problems.

# Comparison table —

ParameterConstant FieldConstant Voltage
Supply Voltage\(1/\alpha\)1
Gate delay\(1/\alpha\)\(1/\alpha^2\)
Power dissipation\(1/\alpha^2\)\(\alpha\)
Power density1\(\alpha^3\)

Module 7: Subsystem Design & Bus Architectures

Structured design processes, 4-bit data path interconnection, bus architectures (1-bus, 2-bus, 3-bus), shifters, parity, and arbitration.

💡 মনে রাখবে | Subsystem & Bus Design Shortcuts
  • Bus Architecture Comparison:
    • 1-Bus Architecture: সবচেয়ে কম এরিয়া ও ওয়্যারিং লাগে, কিন্তু স্পিড কম কারণ প্রতি সাইকেলে মাত্র ১টি ডেটা ট্রান্সফার হতে পারে।
    • 3-Bus Architecture: স্পিড সবচেয়ে বেশি (প্যারালাল সোর্স ও ডেস্টিনেশন রিড-রাইট), কিন্তু এরিয়া ও রাউটিং জটিলতা বেশি।
  • Crossbar vs Barrel Shifter: ক্রসবার সুইচে \(N^2\) টি সুইচ লাগে (বড় এরিয়া)। ব্যারেল শিফটারে শুধুমাত্র \(N \log_2 N\) বা সরাসরি \(N \times N\) বিন্যাস ব্যবহার করে যেকোনো সংখ্যক শিফট এক সাইকেলে করা যায়।
  • Parity Generator Rule: XOR গেটের ল্যাডার স্ট্রাকচার ব্যবহার করে খুব সহজে এবং কম ডিলেতে প্যারিটি চেক করা যায়।
  • Processor Data Path Layout: VLSI চিপে ডেটা পাথ ডিজাইনে রেজিস্টার, ALU এবং শিফটারকে সমান্তরাল অনুভূমিক লাইনে (regular bit-slices) সাজানো হয় যাতে বাস রাউটিং এরিয়া ন্যূনতম হয়। একে স্ট্রাকচার্ড ডাটাপাথ (Structured Datapath) বলে।
  • Shifter Comparison Shortcut: সাধারণ পাস-ট্রানজিস্টর দিয়ে তৈরি শিফটারে সিগন্যাল ক্ষয় হয় (\(V_{th}\) degradation), কিন্তু ট্রান্সমিশন গেট ব্যবহার করলে সিগন্যাল ভোল্টেজ সম্পূর্ণ পাস হয়।

Some problem

Some of the problem associated with VLSI design are:

  1. How to design large complex system in a reasonable time and with reasonable effort. This is a problem shared with other approaches to system design.
  2. The nature of architectures best suited to take full advantage of VLSI and technology.
  3. The testability of large/complex systems once implemented in silicon.

* Problem 1 and 3 are greatly reduced if two aspects of standard practice are accepted:

  1. Approach the design in a top-down manner and with adequate computer-aided tools to do the job. Partition the system sensibly, aiming for simple interconnection between subsystem and high regularity within subsystems. Generate and then verify each section of the design.
  2. Design testability into the system from the outset and be prepared to devote a significant proportion (up to 30%) of the total chip area to test and diagnostic facilities.

# A Illustration of design processes:

  1. Structured design begins with the concept of hierarchy.
  2. It is possible to divide any complex function into less complex subfunction.
  3. As a system's complexity increases, its organization... changes as different factors become relevant to its creation.
  4. Coupling can be used as a measure of how much submodules interact.
  5. It is crucial that components interacting with high frequency be physically proximate, since one may pay severe penalties for long, high-bandwidth interconnects.
  6. Concurrency should be exploited - it is desirable that all gates on the chip do useful work most of the time.
  7. Because technology changes so fast, the adaptation to a new process must occur in a short time. Thus a technology-independent description becomes important.

In represent a design, several approaches may be used at different stages of the design process

  1. Conventional circuit symbols
  2. Logic symbols
  3. Stick diagram
  4. Any mixture of logic symbols and stick diagram that is convenient at a particular stage.
  5. Mask layouts
  6. Architecture block diagram
  7. Floor plans.

The data path has been separated out in Fig-7.2 and it will be seen that the structure comprise a unit which processes data applied at one port and presents it output at a second port.

The two data ports may be combined as a single bidirectional port if storage facilities exist in the data path. Control over the functions to be performed is effected by control signals as indicated.
